## **Saturday, 12:07 p.m. - Grand Prix Atlanta Legacy Primer**

*by Steve Sadin and Blake Rasmussen*

There are a TON of good decks to play in Legacy right now. Whether you want to play a fast combo deck, a beatdown deck, or a control deck – there's going to be a good option for you.

So what are we waiting for? Let's take a look at some decklists!

### Reanimator

While Reanimator wasn't a big factor at Grand Prix Indianapolis (the last Legacy Grand Prix), ever since the release of *Avacyn Restored*, [Animate Dead](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Animate+Dead)s and [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb)s have been running rampant at Legacy tournaments across the globe. It's at the point where many pros have confidently stated that "Reanimator is the deck to beat in Legacy".

And there's one big reason for that – [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand).

The premise behind Reanimator is simple. Get a big creature into your graveyard with [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb), or [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) – then bring it back into play with an [Animate Dead](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Animate+Dead), a [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate), or an [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume).

The deck is blisteringly fast, and thanks to [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze), and [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), it's far from helpless in the face of your opponent's answers.

Plus, even if your opponent has an answer for your reanimated threat (be it a [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares), a [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus), a [Diabolic Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diabolic+Edict), or even a [Pernicious Deed](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pernicious+Deed) with a ton of mana) that won't be good enough... Because you can restock your hand with [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand), you will either draw yourself a [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) to counter their answer, or another reanimation spell to bring forth another huge monster a turn later. And once that's happened, it's only a matter of time before you'll fly to victory.

#### Gerry Thompson Reanimator

Traitors";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}s:4:"type";s:4:"land";s:10:"deck\_count";i:0;}} 

One last important thing to note about the deck: not even uncounterable graveyard hate like [Faerie Macabre](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faerie+Macabre) (which, under normal circumstances, would be a great way to stop a Reanimator deck) isn't enough to beat the current iterations of Legacy Reanimator since they can sideboard in [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell)s to bring huge threats into play without them ever touching the graveyard.

### [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets)

Reanimator may be the deck to beat right now, but tempo oriented [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets) decks are more than capable of holding their own in today's metagame. Shuffling up a mixture of cheap, potent, threats backed by mana denial ([Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) and sometimes [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle)), cheap removal ([Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)s, and [Forked Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forked+Bolt)s), free counterspells ([Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), and [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze)), and [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm)s has been a recipe for success for as long as Legacy has been a format. And right now is no exception.

While the deck is capable of executing a lot of quick kills, it also has a lot of staying power. If you have the time to sculpt your hand with [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm)s and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder)s, and protect your threats with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will)s – then you can beat just about anything with your [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) deck.

### Stoneblade

Tom Martell won Grand Prix Indianapolis just a few months ago with his take on Stoneblade – a Blue-White-Black (or sometimes just Blue-White) deck full of cheap answers that looks to make exceptionally good use of [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic).

While Stoneblade decks might not be as explosive as Reanimator decks which can put [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand)s into play on turn two – the deck is still capable of some very fast starts (there are a lot of decks that simply can't beat a turn three [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull)), and it's almost impossible to hate out with targeted sideboard cards.

So if you're looking for a resilient control deck that's capable of some quick kills – then Stoneblade may very well be the deck for you.

### Other decks with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will)

While Reanimator, RUG [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets), and Stoneblade are currently the most popular blue decks in Legacy, don't be fooled into thinking that they're your only options. There are tons of other great decks with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) in them which (while somewhat out of the public eye right now) are still more than capable of dominating a tournament.

### Sneak and Show

So you want to put a [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand), or an [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) into play on turn two -- but you're worried about [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt)s and friends ruining your day. Well, fear not, because you can circumvent the graveyard entirely by playing Sneak and Show.

Instead of reanimating threats back from the grave, this deck uses [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack), and [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) to put its humongous threats into play. While Sneak and Show decks typically don't have access to black discard spells like [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), they are still more than capable of pushing their threats through with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), [Misdirection](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misdirection), and [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce).

#### Jonathan Hickerson Sneak and Show

### [Hypergenesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypergenesis)

[Hypergenesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypergenesis) is another fun, explosive deck that pops up now and then, but has a hard time gaining too much traction in a format full of decks that can [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) it, or easily counter its namesake spell.

The deck is fairly straightforward. Use various methods of acceleration to power out a fast three-mana cascade spell, cascading into [Hypergenesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypergenesis), the only spell under three mana in the deck. If that resolves, dump all kinds of huge permanents on the board, such as [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn), [Angel of Despair](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Despair), [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) and [Akroma's Memorial](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Akroma%27s+Memorial).

The deck is fun and powerful, but suffers from the vulnerability of its key spell to countermagic and the restrictions on casting costs (for Cascade) that keep it from running disruption like [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) or [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek).

But the deck can be a blast to play and is incredibly powerful if [Hypergenesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypergenesis) resolves. Todd Anderson finished 5th at a SCG Open in Columbus earlier this month with the following list:

#### Todd Anderson Hypergenesis



### Merfolk

Former Legacy standout Merfolk has fallen out of favor recently, but the fact that the deck is full of quick threats (that can grow to huge sizes when surrounded by enough [Lord of Atlantis](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lord+of+Atlantis)es, [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey)s, [Coralhelm Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Coralhelm+Commander)s, and [Phantasmal Image](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phantasmal+Image)s)) backed by the free countermagic suite of [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) and [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), and a great mana base means that Merfolk will always be a viable option.



### Blue White Miracles

If you're looking for an already established archetype that can make good use of the recently unbanned [Land Tax](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Land+Tax) – then look no further than Blue White Miracles. If you want to grind people out with counterspells, clear the board with [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus), and eventually take over the game with a Planeswalker like [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), or a big [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels) – then you're going to have a tough time finding a more appropriate deck than Blue White Control.

Oh, and don't forget that you can always stash away Miracle cards right at the top of your deck with [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), and [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20120630000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top).
